1→"""Thin wrapper around review runner_process for triage stage execution."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import subprocess
     6→import time
     7→from collections.abc import Callable
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.review._runner_process_types import CodexBatchRunnerDeps
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.review.runner_process import (
    12→    codex_batch_command,
    13→    run_codex_batch,
    14→)
    15→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
    16→
    17→
    18→def _output_file_has_text(output_file: Path) -> bool:
    19→    """Return True when the output file exists and contains non-empty text."""
    20→    if not output_file.exists():
    21→        return False
    22→    try:
    23→        return len(output_file.read_text().strip()) > 0
    24→    except OSError:
    25→        return False
    26→
    27→
    28→def run_triage_stage(
    29→    *,
    30→    prompt: str,
    31→    repo_root: Path,
    32→    output_file: Path,
    33→    log_file: Path,
    34→    timeout_seconds: int = 1800,
    35→    validate_output_fn: Callable[[Path], bool] | None = None,
    36→) -> int:
    37→    """Execute a triage stage via codex subprocess. Returns exit code."""
    38→    deps = CodexBatchRunnerDeps(
    39→        timeout_seconds=timeout_seconds,
    40→        subprocess_run=subprocess.run,
    41→        timeout_error=subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
    42→        safe_write_text_fn=safe_write_text,
    43→        use_popen_runner=True,
    44→        subprocess_popen=subprocess.Popen,
    45→        live_log_interval_seconds=10.0,
    46→        stall_after_output_seconds=120,
    47→        max_retries=1,
    48→        retry_backoff_seconds=5.0,
    49→        sleep_fn=time.sleep,
    50→        validate_output_fn=validate_output_fn,
    51→    )
    52→    return run_codex_batch(
    53→        prompt=prompt,
    54→        repo_root=repo_root,
    55→        output_file=output_file,
    56→        log_file=log_file,
    57→        deps=deps,
    58→    )
    59→
    60→
    61→__all__ = ["run_triage_stage"]
    62→

1→"""Helper utilities for plan triage workflow."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→from collections import defaultdict
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_policy import _REVIEW_DETECTORS
    10→from desloppify.engine.plan import (
    11→    TRIAGE_IDS,
    12→    TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS,
    13→    purge_ids,
    14→    review_issue_snapshot_hash,
    15→)
    16→from desloppify.state import utc_now
    17→
    18→from .services import TriageServices, default_triage_services
    19→
    20→_STAGE_ORDER = ["observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich"]
    21→
    22→
    23→def has_triage_in_queue(plan: dict) -> bool:
    24→    """Check if any triage stage ID is in the queue."""
    25→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    26→    return bool(order & TRIAGE_IDS)
    27→
    28→def inject_triage_stages(plan: dict) -> None:
    29→    """Inject all 4 triage stage IDs into the queue (fresh start)."""
    30→    order: list[str] = plan.setdefault("queue_order", [])
    31→    existing = set(order)
    32→    for sid in TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS:
    33→        if sid not in existing:
    34→            order.insert(0 if sid == TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS[0] else len(order), sid)
    35→    # Re-insert in correct order at front
    36→    for sid in reversed(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS):
    37→        if sid in order:
    38→            order.remove(sid)
    39→    insert_at = 0
    40→    for sid in TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS:
    41→        order.insert(insert_at, sid)
    42→        insert_at += 1
    43→
    44→def purge_triage_stage(plan: dict, stage_name: str) -> None:
    45→    """Purge a single triage stage ID from the queue."""
    46→    sid = f"triage::{stage_name}"
    47→    purge_ids(plan, [sid])
    48→
    49→def cascade_clear_later_confirmations(stages: dict, from_stage: str) -> list[str]:
    50→    """Clear confirmed_at/confirmed_text on stages AFTER *from_stage*. Returns cleared names."""
    51→    try:
    52→        idx = _STAGE_ORDER.index(from_stage)
    53→    except ValueError:
    54→        return []
    55→    cleared: list[str] = []
    56→    for later in _STAGE_ORDER[idx + 1:]:
    57→        if later in stages and stages[later].get("confirmed_at"):
    58→            stages[later].pop("confirmed_at", None)
    59→            stages[later].pop("confirmed_text", None)
    60→            cleared.append(later)
    61→    return cleared
    62→
    63→def print_cascade_clear_feedback(cleared: list[str], stages: dict) -> None:
    64→    """Print yellow cascade-clear message with next-step guidance."""
    65→    if not cleared:
    66→        return
    67→    print(colorize(f"  Cleared confirmations on: {', '.join(cleared)}", "yellow"))
    68→    next_unconfirmed = next(
    69→        (s for s in _STAGE_ORDER if s in stages and not stages[s].get("confirmed_at")),
    70→        None,
    71→    )
    72→    if next_unconfirmed:
    73→        print(colorize(
    74→            f"  Re-confirm with: desloppify plan triage --confirm {next_unconfirmed}",
    75→            "dim",
    76→        ))
    77→
    78→def observe_dimension_breakdown(si) -> tuple[dict[str, int], list[str]]:
    79→    """Count issues per dimension from a TriageInput. Returns (by_dim, sorted_dim_names)."""
    80→    by_dim: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
    81→    for _fid, f in si.open_issues.items():
    82→        detail = f.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(f.get("detail"), dict) else {}
    83→        dim = detail.get("dimension", "unknown")
    84→        by_dim[dim] += 1
    85→    dim_names = sorted(by_dim, key=lambda d: (-by_dim[d], d))
    86→    return dict(by_dim), dim_names
    87→
    88→def group_issues_into_observe_batches(
    89→    si,
    90→    max_batches: int = 5,
    91→) -> list[tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict]]]:
    92→    """Group issues by dimension into batches for parallel observe.
    93→
    94→    Returns list of (dimension_names, issues_subset) tuples.
    95→    Single batch if only one dimension exists.
    96→    """
    97→    by_dim, dim_names = observe_dimension_breakdown(si)
    98→
    99→    if len(dim_names) <= 1:
   100→        return [(dim_names, dict(si.open_issues))]
   101→
   102→    # Distribute dimensions into balanced batches by issue count
   103→    num_batches = min(max_batches, len(dim_names))
   104→    batch_dims: list[list[str]] = [[] for _ in range(num_batches)]
   105→    batch_counts: list[int] = [0] * num_batches
   106→
   107→    # Greedy: assign each dimension (largest first) to the lightest batch
   108→    for dim in dim_names:
   109→        lightest = min(range(num_batches), key=lambda i: batch_counts[i])
   110→        batch_dims[lightest].append(dim)
   111→        batch_counts[lightest] += by_dim[dim]
   112→
   113→    # Build issue subsets per batch
   114→    # Pre-index issues by dimension
   115→    dim_to_issues: dict[str, dict[str, dict]] = defaultdict(dict)
   116→    for fid, f in si.open_issues.items():
   117→        detail = f.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(f.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   118→        dim = detail.get("dimension", "unknown")
   119→        dim_to_issues[dim][fid] = f
   120→
   121→    result: list[tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict]]] = []
   122→    for dims in batch_dims:
   123→        if not dims:
   124→            continue
   125→        subset: dict[str, dict] = {}
   126→        for dim in dims:
   127→            subset.update(dim_to_issues.get(dim, {}))
   128→        if subset:
   129→            result.append((dims, subset))
   130→
   131→    return result
   132→
   133→
   134→def open_review_ids_from_state(state: dict) -> set[str]:
   135→    """Return IDs of all open review/concerns issues in state."""
   136→    return {
   137→        fid for fid, f in state.get("issues", {}).items()
   138→        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in _REVIEW_DETECTORS
   139→    }
   140→
   141→def triage_coverage(
   142→    plan: dict,
   143→    open_review_ids: set[str] | None = None,
   144→) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
   145→    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for review issues in triage.
   146→
   147→    When *open_review_ids* is provided, use it as the full set of review
   148→    issues (from state) instead of falling back to queue_order.
   149→    """
   150→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   151→    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
   152→    for c in clusters.values():
   153→        all_cluster_ids.update(c.get("issue_ids", []))
   154→    if open_review_ids is not None:
   155→        review_ids = list(open_review_ids)
   156→    else:
   157→        review_ids = [
   158→            fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
   159→            if not fid.startswith("triage::") and not fid.startswith("workflow::") and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
   160→        ]
   161→    organized = sum(1 for fid in review_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
   162→    return organized, len(review_ids), clusters
   163→
   164→def manual_clusters_with_issues(plan: dict) -> list[str]:
   165→    """Return names of non-auto clusters that have issues."""
   166→    return [
   167→        name for name, c in plan.get("clusters", {}).items()
   168→        if c.get("issue_ids") and not c.get("auto")
   169→    ]
   170→
   171→def apply_completion(
   172→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   173→    plan: dict,
   174→    strategy: str,
   175→    *,
   176→    services: TriageServices | None = None,
   177→) -> None:
   178→    """Shared completion logic: update meta, remove triage::pending, save."""
   179→    resolved_services = services or default_triage_services()
   180→    runtime = resolved_services.command_runtime(args)
   181→    state = runtime.state
   182→
   183→    organized, total, clusters = triage_coverage(
   184→        plan, open_review_ids=open_review_ids_from_state(state),
   185→    )
   186→
   187→    # Purge all triage stage IDs.
   188→    purge_ids(plan, list(TRIAGE_IDS))
   189→
   190→    current_hash = review_issue_snapshot_hash(state)
   191→
   192→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_triage_meta", {})
   193→    meta["issue_snapshot_hash"] = current_hash
   194→    open_ids = sorted(
   195→        fid for fid, f in state.get("issues", {}).items()
   196→        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in _REVIEW_DETECTORS
   197→    )
   198→    meta["triaged_ids"] = open_ids
   199→    if strategy.strip().lower() != "same":
   200→        meta["strategy_summary"] = strategy
   201→    meta["trigger"] = "manual_triage"
   202→    meta["last_completed_at"] = utc_now()
   203→    # Archive stages before clearing so previous analysis is preserved
   204→    stages = meta.get("triage_stages", {})
   205→    if stages:
   206→        meta["last_triage"] = {
   207→            "completed_at": utc_now(),
   208→            "stages": {k: dict(v) for k, v in stages.items()},
   209→            "strategy": strategy if strategy.strip().lower() != "same" else meta.get("strategy_summary", ""),
   210→        }
   211→    meta["triage_stages"] = {}  # clear stages on completion
   212→    meta.pop("stage_refresh_required", None)
   213→    meta.pop("stage_snapshot_hash", None)
   214→
   215→    resolved_services.save_plan(plan)
   216→
   217→    cluster_count = len([c for c in clusters.values() if c.get("issue_ids")])
   218→    print(colorize(f"  Triage complete: {organized}/{total} issues in {cluster_count} cluster(s).", "green"))
   219→    effective_strategy = strategy if strategy.strip().lower() != "same" else meta.get("strategy_summary", "")
   220→    if effective_strategy:
   221→        print(colorize(f"  Strategy: {effective_strategy}", "cyan"))
   222→    print(colorize("  Run `desloppify next` to start implementation.", "green"))
   223→
   224→def find_cluster_for(fid: str, clusters: dict) -> str | None:
   225→    """Return the cluster name containing *fid*, or None."""
   226→    for name, c in clusters.items():
   227→        if fid in c.get("issue_ids", []):
   228→            return name
   229→    return None
   230→
   231→def count_log_activity_since(plan: dict, since: str) -> dict[str, int]:
   232→    """Count execution log entries by action since *since* timestamp."""
   233→    counts: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   234→    for entry in plan.get("execution_log", []):
   235→        if entry.get("timestamp", "") >= since:
   236→            counts[entry.get("action", "unknown")] += 1
   237→    return dict(counts)
   238→
   239→__all__ = [
   240→    "apply_completion",
   241→    "cascade_clear_later_confirmations",
   242→    "count_log_activity_since",
   243→    "find_cluster_for",
   244→    "group_issues_into_observe_batches",
   245→    "has_triage_in_queue",
   246→    "inject_triage_stages",
   247→    "manual_clusters_with_issues",
   248→    "observe_dimension_breakdown",
   249→    "open_review_ids_from_state",
   250→    "print_cascade_clear_feedback",
   251→    "purge_triage_stage",
   252→    "triage_coverage",
   253→]
   254→
```

1→"""Runtime helpers for review batch execution."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→from collections.abc import Callable
     7→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     8→from datetime import UTC, datetime
     9→from functools import partial
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
    12→
    13→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    14→    from .runner_parallel import BatchProgressEvent
    15→
    16→
    17→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    18→class BatchRunSummaryConfig:
    19→    """Inputs required to write the run_summary.json payload."""
    20→
    21→    created_at: str
    22→    run_stamp: str
    23→    runner: str
    24→    run_parallel: bool
    25→    selected_indexes: list[int]
    26→    allow_partial: bool
    27→    max_parallel_batches: int
    28→    batch_timeout_seconds: int
    29→    batch_max_retries: int
    30→    batch_retry_backoff_seconds: float
    31→    heartbeat_seconds: float
    32→    stall_warning_seconds: int
    33→    stall_kill_seconds: int
    34→    immutable_packet_path: Path
    35→    prompt_packet_path: Path
    36→    run_dir: Path
    37→    logs_dir: Path
    38→    run_log_path: Path
    39→    backlog_gate: dict[str, object] | None = None
    40→
    41→
    42→@dataclass
    43→class BatchProgressTracker:
    44→    """Tracks per-batch lifecycle state and emits progress/log events."""
    45→
    46→    selected_indexes: list[int]
    47→    prompt_files: dict[int, Path]
    48→    output_files: dict[int, Path]
    49→    log_files: dict[int, Path]
    50→    total_batches: int
    51→    colorize_fn: Callable[[str, str], str]
    52→    append_run_log_fn: Callable[[str], None]
    53→    stall_warning_seconds: int
    54→    batch_positions: dict[int, int] = field(init=False)
    55→    batch_status: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = field(init=False)
    56→    stall_warned_batches: set[int] = field(default_factory=set, init=False)
    57→
    58→    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
    59→        self.batch_positions = {
    60→            batch_idx: pos + 1 for pos, batch_idx in enumerate(self.selected_indexes)
    61→        }
    62→        self.batch_status = {
    63→            str(idx + 1): {
    64→                "position": self.batch_positions.get(idx, 0),
    65→                "status": "pending",
    66→                "prompt_path": str(self.prompt_files[idx]),
    67→                "result_path": str(self.output_files[idx]),
    68→                "log_path": str(self.log_files[idx]),
    69→            }
    70→            for idx in self.selected_indexes
    71→        }
    72→
    73→    def report(self, batch_index: int, event: str, code: int | None = None, **details) -> None:
    74→        if event == "heartbeat":
    75→            self._report_heartbeat(details)
    76→            return
    77→
    78→        position = self.batch_positions.get(batch_index, 0)
    79→        key = str(batch_index + 1)
    80→        state = self.batch_status.setdefault(
    81→            key,
    82→            {
    83→                "position": position,
    84→                "status": "pending",
    85→                "prompt_path": str(self.prompt_files.get(batch_index, "")),
    86→                "result_path": str(self.output_files.get(batch_index, "")),
    87→                "log_path": str(self.log_files.get(batch_index, "")),
    88→            },
    89→        )
    90→        if event == "queued":
    91→            state["status"] = "queued"
    92→            print(
    93→                self.colorize_fn(
    94→                    f"  Batch {position}/{self.total_batches} queued (#{batch_index + 1})",
    95→                    "dim",
    96→                )
    97→            )
    98→            self.append_run_log_fn(
    99→                f"batch-queued batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{self.total_batches}"
   100→            )
   101→            return
   102→        if event == "start":
   103→            state["status"] = "running"
   104→            state["started_at"] = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   105→            print(
   106→                self.colorize_fn(
   107→                    f"  Batch {position}/{self.total_batches} started (#{batch_index + 1})",
   108→                    "dim",
   109→                )
   110→            )
   111→            self.append_run_log_fn(
   112→                f"batch-start batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{self.total_batches}"
   113→            )
   114→            return
   115→        if event != "done":
   116→            return
   117→        self._mark_done(batch_index, code=code, details=details)
   118→
   119→    def report_event(self, progress_event: BatchProgressEvent) -> None:
   120→        """Typed event entrypoint shared with runner_parallel callbacks."""
   121→        if not hasattr(progress_event, "batch_index") or not hasattr(
   122→            progress_event,
   123→            "event",
   124→        ):
   125→            return
   126→        details = getattr(progress_event, "details", {})
   127→        payload = details if isinstance(details, dict) else {}
   128→        self.report(
   129→            int(getattr(progress_event, "batch_index", -1)),
   130→            str(getattr(progress_event, "event", "")),
   131→            getattr(progress_event, "code", None),
   132→            **payload,
   133→        )
   134→
   135→    def record_execution_issue(self, batch_index: int, exc: Exception) -> None:
   136→        if batch_index < 0:
   137→            self.append_run_log_fn(f"execution-error heartbeat error={exc}")
   138→            return
   139→        self.append_run_log_fn(f"execution-error batch={batch_index + 1} error={exc}")
   140→
   141→    def mark_interrupted(self) -> None:
   142→        for idx in self.selected_indexes:
   143→            key = str(idx + 1)
   144→            state = self.batch_status.setdefault(
   145→                key,
   146→                {"position": self.batch_positions.get(idx, 0), "status": "pending"},
   147→            )
   148→            if state.get("status") in {"pending", "queued", "running"}:
   149→                state["status"] = "interrupted"
   150→
   151→    def mark_final_statuses(
   152→        self,
   153→        *,
   154→        selected_indexes: list[int],
   155→        failure_set: set[int],
   156→        execution_failure_set: set[int],
   157→    ) -> None:
   158→        for idx in selected_indexes:
   159→            key = str(idx + 1)
   160→            state = self.batch_status.setdefault(
   161→                key,
   162→                {"position": self.batch_positions.get(idx, 0), "status": "pending"},
   163→            )
   164→            if idx not in failure_set:
   165→                state["status"] = "succeeded"
   166→                continue
   167→            if idx in execution_failure_set:
   168→                state["status"] = "failed"
   169→                continue
   170→            if not self.output_files[idx].exists():
   171→                state["status"] = "missing_output"
   172→                continue
   173→            state["status"] = "parse_failed"
   174→
   175→    def _report_heartbeat(self, details: dict[str, object]) -> None:
   176→        active, queued, elapsed = _normalize_heartbeat_payload(details)
   177→        if not active and not queued:
   178→            return
   179→        segments = _heartbeat_segments(active, elapsed)
   180→        queued_segment = f", queued {len(queued)}" if queued else ""
   181→        print(
   182→            self.colorize_fn(
   183→                "  Batch heartbeat: "
   184→                f"{len(active)}/{self.total_batches} active{queued_segment} "
   185→                f"({', '.join(segments) if segments else 'running batches pending'})",
   186→                "dim",
   187→            )
   188→        )
   189→        self.append_run_log_fn(
   190→            "heartbeat "
   191→            f"active={[idx + 1 for idx in active]} queued={[idx + 1 for idx in queued]} "
   192→            f"elapsed={{{_heartbeat_elapsed_log(active, elapsed)}}}"
   193→        )
   194→        if self.stall_warning_seconds <= 0:
   195→            return
   196→        slow_active = _slow_active_batches(
   197→            active,
   198→            elapsed=elapsed,
   199→            threshold=self.stall_warning_seconds,
   200→        )
   201→        newly_warned = [idx for idx in slow_active if idx not in self.stall_warned_batches]
   202→        if not newly_warned:
   203→            return
   204→        self.stall_warned_batches.update(newly_warned)
   205→        warning_message = (
   206→            "  Stall warning: batches "
   207→            f"{[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(newly_warned)]} exceeded "
   208→            f"{self.stall_warning_seconds}s elapsed. "
   209→            "This may be normal for long runs; review run.log and batch logs."
   210→        )
   211→        print(self.colorize_fn(warning_message, "yellow"))
   212→        self.append_run_log_fn(
   213→            "stall-warning "
   214→            f"threshold={self.stall_warning_seconds}s "
   215→            f"batches={[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(newly_warned)]}"
   216→        )
   217→
   218→    def _mark_done(self, batch_index: int, *, code: int | None, details: dict[str, object]) -> None:
   219→        position = self.batch_positions.get(batch_index, 0)
   220→        key = str(batch_index + 1)
   221→        state = self.batch_status.setdefault(
   222→            key,
   223→            {
   224→                "position": position,
   225→                "status": "pending",
   226→                "prompt_path": str(self.prompt_files.get(batch_index, "")),
   227→                "result_path": str(self.output_files.get(batch_index, "")),
   228→                "log_path": str(self.log_files.get(batch_index, "")),
   229→            },
   230→        )
   231→        status = "done" if code == 0 else f"failed ({code})"
   232→        tone = "dim" if code == 0 else "yellow"
   233→        elapsed_seconds = details.get("elapsed_seconds")
   234→        elapsed_suffix = ""
   235→        if isinstance(elapsed_seconds, int | float):
   236→            elapsed_suffix = f" in {int(max(0, elapsed_seconds))}s"
   237→            state["elapsed_seconds"] = int(max(0, elapsed_seconds))
   238→        state["status"] = "succeeded" if code == 0 else "failed"
   239→        state["exit_code"] = int(code) if isinstance(code, int) else code
   240→        state["completed_at"] = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   241→        self.stall_warned_batches.discard(batch_index)
   242→        print(
   243→            self.colorize_fn(
   244→                f"  Batch {position}/{self.total_batches} {status}{elapsed_suffix} (#{batch_index + 1})",
   245→                tone,
   246→            )
   247→        )
   248→        self.append_run_log_fn(
   249→            f"batch-done batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{self.total_batches} "
   250→            f"code={code} elapsed={state.get('elapsed_seconds', 0)}"
   251→        )
   252→
   253→
   254→def _normalize_heartbeat_payload(
   255→    details: dict[str, object],
   256→) -> tuple[list[int], list[int], dict[int, object]]:
   257→    active = details.get("active_batches")
   258→    queued = details.get("queued_batches", [])
   259→    elapsed = details.get("elapsed_seconds", {})
   260→    active_list = active if isinstance(active, list) else []
   261→    queued_list = queued if isinstance(queued, list) else []
   262→    elapsed_map = elapsed if isinstance(elapsed, dict) else {}
   263→    return active_list, queued_list, elapsed_map
   264→
   265→
   266→def _elapsed_seconds_for(elapsed: dict[int, object], index: int) -> int:
   267→    raw_secs = elapsed.get(index, 0)
   268→    return int(raw_secs) if isinstance(raw_secs, int | float) else 0
   269→
   270→
   271→def _heartbeat_segments(active: list[int], elapsed: dict[int, object]) -> list[str]:
   272→    segments: list[str] = []
   273→    for idx in active[:6]:
   274→        segments.append(f"#{idx + 1}:{_elapsed_seconds_for(elapsed, idx)}s")
   275→    if len(active) > 6:
   276→        segments.append(f"+{len(active) - 6} more")
   277→    return segments
   278→
   279→
   280→def _heartbeat_elapsed_log(active: list[int], elapsed: dict[int, object]) -> str:
   281→    return ", ".join(f"{idx + 1}:{elapsed.get(idx, 0)}" for idx in active)
   282→
   283→
   284→def _slow_active_batches(
   285→    active: list[int],
   286→    *,
   287→    elapsed: dict[int, object],
   288→    threshold: int,
   289→) -> list[int]:
   290→    return [
   291→        idx
   292→        for idx in active
   293→        if _elapsed_seconds_for(elapsed, idx) >= threshold
   294→    ]
   295→
   296→
   297→def resolve_run_log_path(
   298→    raw_run_log_file: object,
   299→    *,
   300→    project_root: Path,
   301→    run_dir: Path,
   302→) -> Path:
   303→    if isinstance(raw_run_log_file, str) and raw_run_log_file.strip():
   304→        candidate = Path(raw_run_log_file.strip()).expanduser()
   305→        run_log_path = candidate if candidate.is_absolute() else project_root / candidate
   306→    else:
   307→        run_log_path = run_dir / "run.log"
   308→    run_log_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   309→    return run_log_path
   310→
   311→
   312→def make_run_log_writer(run_log_path: Path) -> Callable[[str], None]:
   313→    def _append_run_log(message: str) -> None:
   314→        line = f"{datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec='seconds')} {message}\n"
   315→        try:
   316→            with run_log_path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
   317→                handle.write(line)
   318→        except OSError:
   319→            return
   320→
   321→    return _append_run_log
   322→
   323→
   324→def build_batch_tasks(
   325→    *,
   326→    selected_indexes: list[int],
   327→    prompt_files: dict[int, Path],
   328→    output_files: dict[int, Path],
   329→    log_files: dict[int, Path],
   330→    project_root: Path,
   331→    run_codex_batch_fn: Callable[..., int],
   332→) -> dict[int, Callable[[], int]]:
   333→    return {
   334→        idx: partial(
   335→            _run_batch_task,
   336→            batch_index=idx,
   337→            prompt_path=prompt_files[idx],
   338→            output_path=output_files[idx],
   339→            log_path=log_files[idx],
   340→            project_root=project_root,
   341→            run_codex_batch_fn=run_codex_batch_fn,
   342→        )
   343→        for idx in selected_indexes
   344→    }
   345→
   346→
   347→def write_run_summary(
   348→    *,
   349→    summary_path: Path,
   350→    summary_config: BatchRunSummaryConfig,
   351→    batch_status: dict[str, dict[str, object]],
   352→    successful_batches: list[int],
   353→    failed_batches: list[int],
   354→    safe_write_text_fn: Callable[[Path, str], None],
   355→    colorize_fn: Callable[[str, str], str],
   356→    append_run_log_fn: Callable[[str], None],
   357→    interrupted: bool = False,
   358→    interruption_reason: str | None = None,
   359→) -> None:
   360→    run_summary: dict[str, object] = {
   361→        "created_at": summary_config.created_at,
   362→        "run_stamp": summary_config.run_stamp,
   363→        "runner": summary_config.runner,
   364→        "parallel": summary_config.run_parallel,
   365→        "selected_batches": [idx + 1 for idx in summary_config.selected_indexes],
   366→        "successful_batches": successful_batches,
   367→        "failed_batches": failed_batches,
   368→        "allow_partial": summary_config.allow_partial,
   369→        "max_parallel_batches": (
   370→            summary_config.max_parallel_batches if summary_config.run_parallel else 1
   371→        ),
   372→        "batch_timeout_seconds": summary_config.batch_timeout_seconds,
   373→        "batch_max_retries": summary_config.batch_max_retries,
   374→        "batch_retry_backoff_seconds": summary_config.batch_retry_backoff_seconds,
   375→        "batch_heartbeat_seconds": (
   376→            summary_config.heartbeat_seconds if summary_config.run_parallel else None
   377→        ),
   378→        "batch_stall_warning_seconds": (
   379→            summary_config.stall_warning_seconds if summary_config.run_parallel else None
   380→        ),
   381→        "batch_stall_kill_seconds": summary_config.stall_kill_seconds,
   382→        "immutable_packet": str(summary_config.immutable_packet_path),
   383→        "blind_packet": str(summary_config.prompt_packet_path),
   384→        "run_dir": str(summary_config.run_dir),
   385→        "logs_dir": str(summary_config.logs_dir),
   386→        "run_log": str(summary_config.run_log_path),
   387→        "batches": batch_status,
   388→    }
   389→    if isinstance(summary_config.backlog_gate, dict):
   390→        run_summary["backlog_gate"] = summary_config.backlog_gate
   391→    if interrupted:
   392→        run_summary["interrupted"] = True
   393→        if interruption_reason:
   394→            run_summary["interruption_reason"] = interruption_reason
   395→    safe_write_text_fn(summary_path, json.dumps(run_summary, indent=2) + "\n")
   396→    print(colorize_fn(f"  Run summary: {summary_path}", "dim"))
   397→    append_run_log_fn(f"run-summary {summary_path}")
   398→
   399→
   400→def _run_batch_task(
   401→    *,
   402→    batch_index: int,
   403→    prompt_path: Path,
   404→    output_path: Path,
   405→    log_path: Path,
   406→    project_root: Path,
   407→    run_codex_batch_fn: Callable[..., int],
   408→) -> int:
   409→    try:
   410→        prompt = prompt_path.read_text()
   411→    except OSError as exc:
   412→        raise RuntimeError(
   413→            f"unable to read prompt for batch #{batch_index + 1}: {prompt_path}"
   414→        ) from exc
   415→    return run_codex_batch_fn(
   416→        prompt=prompt,
   417→        repo_root=project_root,
   418→        output_file=output_path,
   419→        log_file=log_path,
   420→    )
   421→
   422→
   423→__all__ = [
   424→    "BatchProgressTracker",
   425→    "BatchRunSummaryConfig",
   426→    "build_batch_tasks",
   427→    "make_run_log_writer",
   428→    "resolve_run_log_path",
   429→    "write_run_summary",
   430→]
   431→

1→"""Parallel execution and progress-callback helpers for review batches."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import threading
     8→from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→
    11→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
    12→
    13→from ._runner_parallel_execution import (
    14→    _drain_parallel_completions,
    15→    _execute_serial,
    16→    _queue_parallel_tasks,
    17→    _resolve_parallel_runtime,
    18→)
    19→from ._runner_parallel_progress import (
    20→    _coerce_batch_execution_options,
    21→)
    22→from ._runner_parallel_types import (
    23→    BatchExecutionOptions,
    24→    BatchProgressEvent,
    25→    BatchResult,
    26→    BatchTask,
    27→)
    28→from .runner_process import _extract_payload_from_log
    29→
    30→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    31→
    32→
    33→def execute_batches(
    34→    *,
    35→    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
    36→    options: BatchExecutionOptions | None = None,
    37→    progress_fn=None,
    38→    error_log_fn=None,
    39→) -> list[int]:
    40→    """Run indexed tasks and return failed index list.
    41→
    42→    Each value in *tasks* is a zero-arg callable returning an int exit code.
    43→    All domain knowledge (files, prompts, etc.) is pre-bound by the caller.
    44→    """
    45→    resolved_options = _coerce_batch_execution_options(options)
    46→    contract_cache: dict[int, str] = {}
    47→    indexes = sorted(tasks)
    48→    if resolved_options.run_parallel:
    49→        max_workers, heartbeat = _resolve_parallel_runtime(
    50→            indexes=indexes,
    51→            max_parallel_workers=resolved_options.max_parallel_workers,
    52→            heartbeat_seconds=resolved_options.heartbeat_seconds,
    53→        )
    54→        failures: set[int] = set()
    55→        progress_failures: set[int] = set()
    56→        started_at: dict[int, float] = {}
    57→        lock = threading.Lock()
    58→        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers) as executor:
    59→            futures = _queue_parallel_tasks(
    60→                executor=executor,
    61→                indexes=indexes,
    62→                tasks=tasks,
    63→                progress_fn=progress_fn,
    64→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    65→                contract_cache=contract_cache,
    66→                max_workers=max_workers,
    67→                failures=failures,
    68→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
    69→                started_at=started_at,
    70→                lock=lock,
    71→                clock_fn=resolved_options.clock_fn,
    72→            )
    73→            pending = set(futures.keys())
    74→            _drain_parallel_completions(
    75→                pending=pending,
    76→                futures=futures,
    77→                heartbeat=heartbeat,
    78→                indexes=indexes,
    79→                progress_fn=progress_fn,
    80→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    81→                contract_cache=contract_cache,
    82→                failures=failures,
    83→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
    84→                started_at=started_at,
    85→                lock=lock,
    86→                clock_fn=resolved_options.clock_fn,
    87→            )
    88→        return sorted(failures)
    89→    return _execute_serial(
    90→        tasks=tasks,
    91→        indexes=indexes,
    92→        progress_fn=progress_fn,
    93→        error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    94→        clock_fn=resolved_options.clock_fn,
    95→        contract_cache=contract_cache,
    96→    )
    97→
    98→
    99→def collect_batch_results(
   100→    *,
   101→    selected_indexes: list[int],
   102→    failures: list[int],
   103→    output_files: dict[int, Path],
   104→    allowed_dims: set[str],
   105→    extract_payload_fn,
   106→    normalize_result_fn,
   107→) -> tuple[list[BatchResult], list[int]]:
   108→    """Parse and normalize batch outputs, preserving prior failures."""
   109→    batch_results: list[BatchResult] = []
   110→    failure_set = set(failures)
   111→    for idx in selected_indexes:
   112→        had_execution_failure = idx in failure_set
   113→        raw_path = output_files[idx]
   114→        payload = None
   115→        parsed_from_log = False
   116→        if raw_path.exists():
   117→            try:
   118→                payload = extract_payload_fn(raw_path.read_text())
   119→            except OSError as exc:
   120→                logger.warning("Failed reading batch payload %s: %s", raw_path, exc)
   121→                payload = None
   122→        if payload is None:
   123→            payload = _extract_payload_from_log(idx, raw_path, extract_payload_fn)
   124→            parsed_from_log = payload is not None
   125→        if payload is None:
   126→            failure_set.add(idx)
   127→            continue
   128→        if parsed_from_log:
   129→            try:
   130→                safe_write_text(raw_path, json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
   131→            except OSError as exc:
   132→                logger.warning("Failed writing normalized batch payload %s: %s", raw_path, exc)
   133→        try:
   134→            assessments, issues, dimension_notes, quality = normalize_result_fn(
   135→                payload,
   136→                allowed_dims,
   137→            )
   138→        except ValueError as exc:
   139→            logger.debug("Invalid batch payload at index %s (%s): %s", idx, raw_path, exc)
   140→            failure_set.add(idx)
   141→            continue
   142→        if had_execution_failure:
   143→            failure_set.discard(idx)
   144→        batch_results.append(
   145→            BatchResult(
   146→                batch_index=idx + 1,
   147→                assessments=assessments,
   148→                dimension_notes=dimension_notes,
   149→                issues=issues,
   150→                quality=quality,
   151→            )
   152→        )
   153→    return batch_results, sorted(failure_set)
   154→
   155→
   156→__all__ = [
   157→    "BatchResult",
   158→    "BatchExecutionOptions",
   159→    "BatchProgressEvent",
   160→    "collect_batch_results",
   161→    "execute_batches",
   162→]
   163→

1→"""Serial/parallel execution loops for review batch tasks."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import threading
     7→from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
     8→from concurrent.futures import TimeoutError as FuturesTimeoutError
     9→
    10→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure
    11→
    12→from ._runner_parallel_progress import (
    13→    _RUNNER_CALLBACK_EXCEPTIONS,
    14→    _RUNNER_TASK_EXCEPTIONS,
    15→    _emit_progress,
    16→    _record_execution_error,
    17→    _record_progress_error,
    18→)
    19→from ._runner_parallel_types import BatchTask
    20→
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→
    24→def _execute_serial(
    25→    *,
    26→    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
    27→    indexes: list[int],
    28→    progress_fn,
    29→    error_log_fn,
    30→    clock_fn,
    31→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
    32→) -> list[int]:
    33→    """Run tasks one at a time — no threads, no closures."""
    34→    failures: set[int] = set()
    35→    for idx in indexes:
    36→        t0 = float(clock_fn())
    37→        start_error = _emit_progress(
    38→            progress_fn,
    39→            idx,
    40→            "start",
    41→            None,
    42→            details={"max_workers": 1},
    43→            contract_cache=contract_cache,
    44→        )
    45→        if start_error is not None:
    46→            _record_execution_error(
    47→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    48→                failures=failures,
    49→                idx=idx,
    50→                exc=start_error,
    51→            )
    52→        try:
    53→            code = tasks[idx]()
    54→        except _RUNNER_TASK_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
    55→            _record_execution_error(
    56→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    57→                failures=failures,
    58→                idx=idx,
    59→                exc=exc,
    60→            )
    61→            code = 1
    62→        if code != 0:
    63→            failures.add(idx)
    64→        done_error = _emit_progress(
    65→            progress_fn,
    66→            idx,
    67→            "done",
    68→            code,
    69→            details={"elapsed_seconds": int(max(0.0, clock_fn() - t0))},
    70→            contract_cache=contract_cache,
    71→        )
    72→        if done_error is not None:
    73→            _record_execution_error(
    74→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    75→                failures=failures,
    76→                idx=idx,
    77→                exc=done_error,
    78→            )
    79→    return sorted(failures)
    80→
    81→
    82→def _resolve_parallel_runtime(
    83→    *,
    84→    indexes: list[int],
    85→    max_parallel_workers,
    86→    heartbeat_seconds,
    87→) -> tuple[int, float | None]:
    88→    requested = (
    89→        int(max_parallel_workers)
    90→        if isinstance(max_parallel_workers, int) and max_parallel_workers > 0
    91→        else 8
    92→    )
    93→    max_workers = max(1, min(len(indexes), requested))
    94→    heartbeat = (
    95→        float(heartbeat_seconds)
    96→        if isinstance(heartbeat_seconds, int | float) and heartbeat_seconds > 0
    97→        else None
    98→    )
    99→    return max_workers, heartbeat
   100→
   101→
   102→def _run_parallel_task(
   103→    *,
   104→    idx: int,
   105→    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
   106→    progress_fn,
   107→    error_log_fn,
   108→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
   109→    max_workers: int,
   110→    progress_failures: set[int],
   111→    started_at: dict[int, float],
   112→    lock: threading.Lock,
   113→    clock_fn,
   114→) -> int:
   115→    with lock:
   116→        started_at[idx] = float(clock_fn())
   117→    progress_error = _emit_progress(
   118→        progress_fn,
   119→        idx,
   120→        "start",
   121→        None,
   122→        details={"max_workers": max_workers},
   123→        contract_cache=contract_cache,
   124→    )
   125→    if progress_error is not None:
   126→        _record_progress_error(
   127→            idx=idx,
   128→            err=progress_error,
   129→            progress_failures=progress_failures,
   130→            lock=lock,
   131→            error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   132→        )
   133→    return tasks[idx]()
   134→
   135→
   136→def _queue_parallel_tasks(
   137→    *,
   138→    executor: ThreadPoolExecutor,
   139→    indexes: list[int],
   140→    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
   141→    progress_fn,
   142→    error_log_fn,
   143→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
   144→    max_workers: int,
   145→    failures: set[int],
   146→    progress_failures: set[int],
   147→    started_at: dict[int, float],
   148→    lock: threading.Lock,
   149→    clock_fn,
   150→) -> dict:
   151→    futures: dict = {}
   152→    for idx in indexes:
   153→        queue_error = _emit_progress(
   154→            progress_fn,
   155→            idx,
   156→            "queued",
   157→            None,
   158→            details={"max_workers": max_workers},
   159→            contract_cache=contract_cache,
   160→        )
   161→        if queue_error is not None:
   162→            _record_progress_error(
   163→                idx=idx,
   164→                err=queue_error,
   165→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
   166→                lock=lock,
   167→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   168→            )
   169→            failures.add(idx)
   170→        futures[
   171→            executor.submit(
   172→                _run_parallel_task,
   173→                idx=idx,
   174→                tasks=tasks,
   175→                progress_fn=progress_fn,
   176→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   177→                contract_cache=contract_cache,
   178→                max_workers=max_workers,
   179→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
   180→                started_at=started_at,
   181→                lock=lock,
   182→                clock_fn=clock_fn,
   183→            )
   184→        ] = idx
   185→    return futures
   186→
   187→
   188→def _complete_parallel_future(
   189→    *,
   190→    future,
   191→    futures: dict,
   192→    progress_fn,
   193→    error_log_fn,
   194→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
   195→    failures: set[int],
   196→    progress_failures: set[int],
   197→    started_at: dict[int, float],
   198→    lock: threading.Lock,
   199→    clock_fn,
   200→) -> None:
   201→    idx = futures[future]
   202→    with lock:
   203→        t0 = started_at.get(idx, float(clock_fn()))
   204→    elapsed = int(max(0.0, clock_fn() - t0))
   205→    try:
   206→        code = future.result()
   207→    except _RUNNER_TASK_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
   208→        _record_execution_error(
   209→            error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   210→            failures=failures,
   211→            idx=idx,
   212→            exc=exc,
   213→        )
   214→        done_error = _emit_progress(
   215→            progress_fn,
   216→            idx,
   217→            "done",
   218→            1,
   219→            details={"elapsed_seconds": elapsed},
   220→            contract_cache=contract_cache,
   221→        )
   222→        if done_error is not None:
   223→            _record_progress_error(
   224→                idx=idx,
   225→                err=done_error,
   226→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
   227→                lock=lock,
   228→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   229→            )
   230→        return
   231→
   232→    done_error = _emit_progress(
   233→        progress_fn,
   234→        idx,
   235→        "done",
   236→        code,
   237→        details={"elapsed_seconds": elapsed},
   238→        contract_cache=contract_cache,
   239→    )
   240→    if done_error is not None:
   241→        _record_progress_error(
   242→            idx=idx,
   243→            err=done_error,
   244→            progress_failures=progress_failures,
   245→            lock=lock,
   246→            error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   247→        )
   248→
   249→    with lock:
   250→        had_progress_failure = idx in progress_failures
   251→    if code != 0 or had_progress_failure:
   252→        failures.add(idx)
   253→
   254→
   255→def _drain_parallel_completions(
   256→    *,
   257→    pending: set,
   258→    futures: dict,
   259→    heartbeat: float | None,
   260→    indexes: list[int],
   261→    progress_fn,
   262→    error_log_fn,
   263→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
   264→    failures: set[int],
   265→    progress_failures: set[int],
   266→    started_at: dict[int, float],
   267→    lock: threading.Lock,
   268→    clock_fn,
   269→) -> None:
   270→    if heartbeat is None:
   271→        for future in as_completed(pending):
   272→            _complete_parallel_future(
   273→                future=future,
   274→                futures=futures,
   275→                progress_fn=progress_fn,
   276→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   277→                contract_cache=contract_cache,
   278→                failures=failures,
   279→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
   280→                started_at=started_at,
   281→                lock=lock,
   282→                clock_fn=clock_fn,
   283→            )
   284→        return
   285→
   286→    while pending:
   287→        try:
   288→            future = next(as_completed(pending, timeout=heartbeat))
   289→        except FuturesTimeoutError:
   290→            _heartbeat(
   291→                pending,
   292→                futures,
   293→                started_at,
   294→                lock,
   295→                indexes,
   296→                progress_fn,
   297→                clock_fn,
   298→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   299→                contract_cache=contract_cache,
   300→            )
   301→            continue
   302→        pending.discard(future)
   303→        _complete_parallel_future(
   304→            future=future,
   305→            futures=futures,
   306→            progress_fn=progress_fn,
   307→            error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   308→            contract_cache=contract_cache,
   309→            failures=failures,
   310→            progress_failures=progress_failures,
   311→            started_at=started_at,
   312→            lock=lock,
   313→            clock_fn=clock_fn,
   314→        )
   315→
   316→
   317→def _heartbeat(
   318→    pending,
   319→    futures,
   320→    started_at,
   321→    lock,
   322→    indexes,
   323→    progress_fn,
   324→    clock_fn,
   325→    *,
   326→    error_log_fn=None,
   327→    contract_cache: dict[int, str] | None = None,
   328→):
   329→    """Build and emit a heartbeat with active/queued batch status."""
   330→    with lock:
   331→        active = sorted(futures[f] for f in pending if futures[f] in started_at)
   332→    active_set = set(active)
   333→    queued = sorted(futures[f] for f in pending if futures[f] not in active_set)
   334→    elapsed = {
   335→        idx: int(max(0.0, clock_fn() - started_at.get(idx, clock_fn())))
   336→        for idx in active
   337→    }
   338→    heartbeat_error = _emit_progress(
   339→        progress_fn,
   340→        -1,
   341→        "heartbeat",
   342→        None,
   343→        details={
   344→            "active_batches": active,
   345→            "queued_batches": queued,
   346→            "elapsed_seconds": elapsed,
   347→            "active_count": len(active),
   348→            "queued_count": len(queued),
   349→            "total_count": len(indexes),
   350→        },
   351→        contract_cache=contract_cache,
   352→    )
   353→    if heartbeat_error is not None and callable(error_log_fn):
   354→        try:
   355→            error_log_fn(-1, heartbeat_error)
   356→        except _RUNNER_CALLBACK_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
   357→            log_best_effort_failure(
   358→                logger,
   359→                "record batch heartbeat failure via callback",
   360→                exc,
   361→            )
   362→
   363→
   364→__all__ = [
   365→    "_complete_parallel_future",
   366→    "_drain_parallel_completions",
   367→    "_execute_serial",
   368→    "_heartbeat",
   369→    "_queue_parallel_tasks",
   370→    "_resolve_parallel_runtime",
   371→    "_run_parallel_task",
   372→]
   373→
```

    34→def _coerce_batch_execution_options(
    35→    options: BatchExecutionOptions | None = None,
    36→) -> BatchExecutionOptions:
    37→    """Resolve execution options from the typed dataclass contract."""
    38→    base = options or BatchExecutionOptions(run_parallel=False)
    39→    max_parallel_workers = (
    40→        int(base.max_parallel_workers)
    41→        if isinstance(base.max_parallel_workers, int)
    42→        and not isinstance(base.max_parallel_workers, bool)

    49→        else None
    50→    )
    51→    clock_fn = base.clock_fn if callable(base.clock_fn) else time.monotonic
    52→

    54→        run_parallel=bool(base.run_parallel),
    55→        max_parallel_workers=max_parallel_workers,
    56→        heartbeat_seconds=heartbeat_seconds,
    57→        clock_fn=clock_fn,
    58→    )

    60→
    61→def _progress_contract(
    62→    progress_fn,
    63→    *,
    64→    contract_cache: dict[int, str] | None = None,
    65→) -> str:
    66→    """Resolve callback contract once: event callback or none."""
    67→    if not callable(progress_fn):
    68→        return "none"
    69→    fn_id = id(progress_fn)
    70→    cache = contract_cache if contract_cache is not None else {}
    71→    cached = cache.get(fn_id)
    72→    if cached:
    73→        return cached
    74→    contract = "event"
    75→    cache[fn_id] = contract

Perfect! I now have all the files. Let me provide you with a comprehensive analysis of the triage stage patterns.

## Triage Stage Patterns - Complete Analysis

### 1. **Stage Recording Pattern** (`_stage_records.py`)

Each stage record follows this structure:

```python
stages["<stage_name>"] = {
    "stage": "<stage_name>",
    "report": <str>,           # Required: the human analysis
    "timestamp": utc_now(),    # When recorded
    "cited_ids": <list>,       # For observe: issue IDs referenced
    "issue_count": <int>,      # For observe/organize: count of issues
    "shallow_count": <int>,    # For enrich: count of steps without detail
    "confirmed_at": <str>,     # Optional: UTC timestamp when confirmed
    "confirmed_text": <str>,   # Optional: attestation text from --confirm
}
```

**Key rules:**
- `_resolve_reusable_report()` allows reusing a prior report if the stage isn't changing (no new data)
- When a report is reused, `confirmed_at` and `confirmed_text` are preserved
- When a report changes, confirmations on that stage AND all later stages cascade-clear via `cascade_clear_later_confirmations()`
- Each `_record_*` function is specialized: `_record_observe_stage`, `_record_organize_stage`, `_record_enrich_stage`

### 2. **inject_triage_stages Pattern** (`helpers.py`)

```python
def inject_triage_stages(plan: dict) -> None:
    """Inject all 4 triage stage IDs into the queue (fresh start)."""
```

**How it works:**
- Inserts `TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS` into `plan["queue_order"]` at the front (index 0)
- The stage IDs are: `"triage::observe"`, `"triage::reflect"`, `"triage::organize"`, `"triage::enrich"`
- Defined as `["observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich"]` in `_STAGE_ORDER`
- These are **not normal issues** — they're special markers that:
  - Block the queue until explicitly confirmed
  - Get removed by `purge_triage_stage()` or `apply_completion()`
  - Are checked with `TRIAGE_IDS` set in `desloppify/engine/plan.py`

### 3. **purge_triage_stage Pattern** (`helpers.py`)

```python
def purge_triage_stage(plan: dict, stage_name: str) -> None:
    """Purge a single triage stage ID from the queue."""
    sid = f"triage::{stage_name}"
    purge_ids(plan, [sid])
```

**What it does:**
- Removes a single stage ID from `queue_order`
- Used after a stage is confirmed (to let next stage proceed)
- Full cleanup happens in `apply_completion()` which purges all `TRIAGE_IDS`

### 4. **Dashboard Stage Progress Display** (`display.py`, `print_stage_progress()`)

Shows progress with 3 states per stage:

```python
def print_stage_progress(stages: dict, plan: dict | None = None) -> None:
    for stage_name, label in TRIAGE_STAGE_LABELS:
        if stage_name in stages:
            if stages[stage_name].get("confirmed_at"):
                print(f"✓ {label} (confirmed)")      # Green: done + confirmed
            else:
                print(f"✓ {label} (needs confirm)")  # Yellow: recorded, needs confirmation
        elif TRIAGE_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES[stage_name].issubset(stages):
            print(f"→ {label} (current)")            # Yellow: ready to work on
        else:
            print(f"○ {label}")                      # Dim: blocked by prior stages
```

**Key metadata:**
- `TRIAGE_STAGE_LABELS`: Maps stage name → human label (in `triage_playbook.py`)
- `TRIAGE_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES`: Dict showing which stages must complete first (dict of sets)
- Status is `"confirmed"` only if `stages["<name>"].get("confirmed_at")` is set

### 5. **Validation Helpers Available** (`_stage_validation.py`)

Reusable validation patterns:

| Function | Purpose | Pattern |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `_validate_stage_report()` | Check report presence/length with custom guidance | Generic: takes `min_chars`, `missing_guidance`, `short_guidance` |
| `_require_*_stage_for_*()` | Gate: block until prior stage done | E.g., `_require_reflect_stage_for_organize()` |
| `_auto_confirm_*_if_attested()` | Skip confirmation if `--attestation` provided | Validates attestation with `_validate_attestation()` |
| `_*_report_or_error()` | Parse/validate report, print guidance | Specialized per stage (observe, organize, enrich) |
| `_manual_clusters_with_issues()` | Find clusters ready for work | Returns list of non-auto cluster names |
| `_unenriched_clusters()` | Find gaps in cluster metadata | Returns list of (name, missing_fields) |
| `_unclustered_review_issues_or_error()` | Ensure all review issues are in a cluster | Blocks if any open review issue not clustered |

### 6. **Stage Dependency Chain**

From `_STAGE_ORDER = ["observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich"]`:

```
observe → (auto-confirm: --attestation option)
  ↓
confirm observe
  ↓
reflect → (auto-confirm: --attestation option)
  ↓
confirm reflect
  ↓
organize → (requires manual clusters enriched)
  ↓
confirm organize
  ↓
enrich → (requires steps have detail/issue_refs)
  ↓
confirm enrich
  ↓
complete → (purge all TRIAGE_IDS, archive stages to last_triage)
```

### 7. **Cascade Clear Behavior** (`helpers.py`)

```python
def cascade_clear_later_confirmations(stages: dict, from_stage: str) -> list[str]:
    """Clear confirmed_at/confirmed_text on stages AFTER *from_stage*."""
```

When you update a stage:
- It invalidates all downstream confirmations
- Returns list of cleared stage names
- Printed to user with next-step guidance

### 8. **Manual Clusters with Issues** (`helpers.py`)

```python
def manual_clusters_with_issues(plan: dict) -> list[str]:
    """Return names of non-auto clusters that have issues."""
```

Used by:
- `_organize_report_or_error()` — requires at least one manual cluster
- `_auto_confirm_*()` — validates cluster names in attestation
- `print_organize_result()` — shows cluster summary

### 9. **Key Data Flow for New Stage**

To add a new triage stage (hypothetically `"validate"`):

1. **Add to stage order** (in `_STAGE_ORDER` and `TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS`):
   ```python
   _STAGE_ORDER = ["observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich", "validate"]
   TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS = ["triage::observe", "triage::reflect", "triage::organize", "triage::enrich", "triage::validate"]
   ```

2. **Create recording function** in `_stage_records.py`:
   ```python
   def _record_validate_stage(
       stages: dict,
       *,
       report: str,
       some_count: int,
       existing_stage: dict | None,
       is_reuse: bool,
   ) -> list[str]:
       stages["validate"] = {
           "stage": "validate",
           "report": report,
           "timestamp": utc_now(),
           "some_count": some_count,
       }
       if is_reuse and existing_stage and existing_stage.get("confirmed_at"):
           stages["validate"]["confirmed_at"] = existing_stage["confirmed_at"]
           stages["validate"]["confirmed_text"] = existing_stage.get("confirmed_text", "")
       return cascade_clear_later_confirmations(stages, "validate")
   ```

3. **Add validation gates** in command handler:
   ```python
   if not _require_enrich_stage_for_validate(stages):
       return
   ```

4. **Add display label** in `triage_playbook.py`:
   ```python
   TRIAGE_STAGE_LABELS = [
       ("observe", "Observe issues"),
       ("reflect", "Reflect on strategy"),
       ("organize", "Organize into clusters"),
       ("enrich", "Enrich steps"),
       ("validate", "Validate solution"),
   ]
   ```

5. **Call purge after confirmation**:
   ```python
   purge_triage_stage(plan, "validate")
   ```

---

**Summary:** The pattern is declarative — stages are metadata with reports, timestamps, and confirmation states. The orchestration (gates, cascade-clears, purging) is handled by helper functions imported into the command handler. Each stage is atomic (one record function, one validation gate, one label).

Perfect! Now I have a complete understanding of the parallel batch execution pattern. Let me provide a comprehensive report of the exact pattern used:

## Parallel Batch Execution Pattern Report

Based on my analysis of the observe stage implementation and the underlying batch execution infrastructure, here's the exact pattern you'll reuse for the sense-check stage:

### 1. **Batch Definition Data Structure**

Batches are defined as a list of tuples containing:
```python
list[tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict]]]
```

Each tuple contains:
- **First element**: `list[str]` — dimension names grouped together in this batch
- **Second element**: `dict[str, dict]` — subset of issues for this batch (by ID)

**Example from `group_issues_into_observe_batches()`**:
- Groups issues by their "dimension" detail field
- Distributes dimensions across a maximum of 5 batches (configurable)
- Uses greedy load-balancing: assigns each dimension (largest first) to the lightest batch
- Returns single batch if only one dimension exists

### 2. **Parallel Execution API: `execute_batches()`**

**Location**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_parallel.py`

**Function signature**:
```python
def execute_batches(
    *,
    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
    options: BatchExecutionOptions | None = None,
    progress_fn=None,
    error_log_fn=None,
) -> list[int]:  # Returns list of failed batch indices
```

**Parameters**:
- `tasks`: Dictionary mapping batch index (0-based `int`) to `BatchTask` (zero-arg callable returning `int` exit code)
- `options`: `BatchExecutionOptions` dataclass with:
  - `run_parallel: bool` — enable/disable parallelization
  - `max_parallel_workers: int | None` — workers (default 8, capped at number of batches)
  - `heartbeat_seconds: float | None` — progress event interval (default 15.0s)
  - `clock_fn: Callable[[], float]` — time function (default `time.monotonic`)
- `progress_fn`: Optional callback receiving `BatchProgressEvent` objects
- `error_log_fn`: Optional callback for errors: `error_log_fn(batch_index: int, exc: Exception)`

**Returns**: List of batch indices (0-based) that failed

### 3. **Progress Events: `BatchProgressEvent`**

**Location**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/_runner_parallel_types.py`

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BatchProgressEvent:
    batch_index: int           # 0-based batch index
    event: str                 # "queued", "start", "done", "heartbeat"
    code: int | None           # Exit code (None for non-done events)
    details: dict[str, Any]    # Event-specific metadata
```

**Event types**:
- **"queued"**: Batch queued for execution
- **"start"**: Batch execution started (details: `{"max_workers": int}`)
- **"done"**: Batch execution completed (details: `{"elapsed_seconds": int}`, code is exit code)
- **"heartbeat"**: Periodic status update every N seconds (details below)

**Heartbeat details**:
```python
{
    "active_batches": list[int],      # Indices of running batches
    "queued_batches": list[int],      # Indices waiting to start
    "elapsed_seconds": dict[int, int], # Per-batch elapsed times
    "active_count": int,
    "queued_count": int,
    "total_count": int,
}
```

### 4. **Result Merging Pattern**

**Location**: `orchestrator.py` lines 110-127, `_merge_observe_outputs()`

```python
def _merge_observe_outputs(
    batch_outputs: list[tuple[list[str], Path]],
) -> str:
    """Concatenate batch outputs with dimension headers into single observe report."""
    parts: list[str] = []
    for dims, output_file in batch_outputs:
        header = f"## Dimensions: {', '.join(dims)}"
        content = ""
        if output_file.exists():
            try:
                content = output_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").strip()
            except OSError:
                content = "(batch output missing)"
        if not content:
            content = "(batch produced no output)"
        parts.append(f"{header}\n\n{content}")
    return "\n\n---\n\n".join(parts)
```

**Pattern**:
1. Iterate through batch metadata (preserving order)
2. Read each batch's output file
3. Prepend dimension/classifier header
4. Join all with separator (`---`)
5. Return merged string

### 5. **Error/Failure Handling**

**Failures at three levels**:

1. **Execution failures** (exit code != 0):
   - Added to `failures` set during execution
   - Returned as sorted list from `execute_batches()`

2. **Progress callback failures**:
   - Tracked separately in `progress_failures` set
   - Also added to final failures
   - Logged via `error_log_fn(batch_index, exc)`

3. **Error recovery**:
   - **Parallel path**: Fails fast — aborts entire pipeline if `failures` is non-empty (orchestrator.py lines 226-235)
   - **Serial path**: Continues through all batches, accumulating failures
   - Pipeline abort message: `print(colorize(f"  Observe: {len(failures)} batch(es) failed: {failures}", "red"))`

### 6. **Orchestrator Integration Pattern**

**Location**: `orchestrator.py` lines 129-241, `_run_observe()`

**Full workflow**:

```python
def _run_observe(
    *,
    si,                      # TriageInput: issues grouped by dimension
    repo_root: Path,
    prompts_dir: Path,       # Where to write prompts
    output_dir: Path,        # Where subprocesses write outputs
    logs_dir: Path,          # Where subprocess logs go
    timeout_seconds: int,
    dry_run: bool = False,
    append_run_log=None,     # Progress logger
) -> tuple[bool, str]:      # (ok, merged_report)
```

**Steps**:

1. **Split into batches** (lines 152-189):
   ```python
   batches = group_issues_into_observe_batches(si)
   # For each batch: build prompt, write to file, create task
   tasks: dict[int, object] = {}
   batch_meta: list[tuple[list[str], Path]] = []  # Track dims + output paths
   
   for i, (dims, issues_subset) in enumerate(batches):
       prompt = build_observe_batch_prompt(...)
       safe_write_text(prompts_dir / f"observe_batch_{i}.md", prompt)
       output_file = output_dir / f"observe_batch_{i}.raw.txt"
       batch_meta.append((dims, output_file))
       
       tasks[i] = partial(
           run_triage_stage,
           prompt=prompt,
           repo_root=repo_root,
           output_file=output_file,
           log_file=logs_dir / f"observe_batch_{i}.log",
           timeout_seconds=timeout_seconds,
           validate_output_fn=_output_file_has_text,
       )
   ```

2. **Define progress callback** (lines 196-213):
   ```python
   def _progress(event: BatchProgressEvent) -> None:
       idx = event.batch_index
       if event.event == "start":
           print(colorize(f"    Observe batch {idx + 1}/{total} started", "dim"))
           _log(f"observe-batch-start batch={idx + 1}")
       elif event.event == "done":
           elapsed = event.details.get("elapsed_seconds", 0) if event.details else 0
           status = "done" if event.code == 0 else f"failed ({event.code})"
           tone = "dim" if event.code == 0 else "yellow"
           print(colorize(f"    Observe batch {idx + 1}/{total} {status} in {int(elapsed)}s", tone))
           _log(f"observe-batch-done batch={idx + 1} code={event.code} elapsed={int(elapsed)}s")
       elif event.event == "heartbeat":
           details = event.details or {}
           active = details.get("active_batches", [])
           elapsed_map = details.get("elapsed_seconds", {})
           if active:
               parts = [f"#{i + 1}:{int(elapsed_map.get(i, 0))}s" for i in active[:6]]
               print(colorize(f"    Observe heartbeat: {len(active)}/{total} active ({', '.join(parts)})", "dim"))
   ```

3. **Define error logger** (lines 215-216):
   ```python
   def _error_log(batch_index: int, exc: Exception) -> None:
       _log(f"observe-batch-error batch={batch_index + 1} error={exc}")
   ```

4. **Execute in parallel** (lines 219-224):
   ```python
   failures = execute_batches(
       tasks=tasks,
       options=BatchExecutionOptions(run_parallel=True, heartbeat_seconds=15.0),
       progress_fn=_progress,
       error_log_fn=_error_log,
   )
   ```

5. **Handle failures** (lines 226-235):
   ```python
   if failures:
       print(colorize(f"  Observe: {len(failures)} batch(es) failed: {failures}", "red"))
       for idx in failures:
           log_file = logs_dir / f"observe_batch_{idx}.log"
           print(colorize(f"    Check log: {log_file}", "dim"))
       _log(f"observe-parallel-failed failures={failures}")
       return False, ""  # Abort pipeline
   ```

6. **Merge and return** (lines 237-240):
   ```python
   merged = _merge_observe_outputs(batch_meta)
   print(colorize(f"  Observe: merged {total} batch outputs ({len(merged)} chars).", "green"))
   _log(f"observe-parallel-done merged_chars={len(merged)}")
   return True, merged
   ```

### 7. **Subprocess Execution: `run_triage_stage()`**

**Location**: `codex_runner.py` lines 28-58

- Wraps `run_codex_batch()` with `CodexBatchRunnerDeps`
- Returns `int` exit code (0 = success, nonzero = failure)
- Accepts optional `validate_output_fn: Callable[[Path], bool]` to validate output existence
- Uses subprocess.Popen for live log streaming (10s interval) with 120s stall timeout

### 8. **Run Log Integration**

**Location**: `batches_runtime.py` lines 312-321, `make_run_log_writer()`

```python
def make_run_log_writer(run_log_path: Path) -> Callable[[str], None]:
    def _append_run_log(message: str) -> None:
        line = f"{datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec='seconds')} {message}\n"
        try:
            with run_log_path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
                handle.write(line)
        except OSError:
            return
    return _append_run_log
```

**Log entries used**:
- `observe-parallel batches={total}`
- `observe-batch batch={i+1} issues={count} dims={dims}`
- `observe-batch-start batch={i+1}`
- `observe-batch-done batch={i+1} code={code} elapsed={seconds}s`
- `observe-batch-error batch={i+1} error={exc}`
- `observe-parallel-failed failures={list}`
- `observe-parallel-done merged_chars={length}`

---

## Summary for Sense-Check Stage Implementation

To reuse this pattern for sense-check stage with per-cluster sub-subagents:

1. **Define clusters as batches**: Each cluster becomes a batch with issue subset
2. **Build task dict**: Map cluster index to `partial(run_triage_stage, ...)`
3. **Call `execute_batches()`**: Pass tasks + progress callback
4. **Merge results**: Concatenate cluster outputs with headers
5. **Error handling**: Check failures list; abort on any non-empty failures set
6. **Run log**: Use `make_run_log_writer()` for timestamped event logging

The pattern is **fully generic and reusable** — swap dimension grouping for cluster selection, and everything else stays the same.

1→"""Plan persistence — load/save with atomic writes."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import shutil
     8→import sys
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→
    11→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
    12→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure
    13→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
    14→    PLAN_VERSION,
    15→    PlanModel,
    16→    empty_plan,
    17→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    18→    validate_plan,
    19→)
    20→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import STATE_DIR, json_default, utc_now
    21→
    22→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    23→
    24→PLAN_FILE = STATE_DIR / "plan.json"
    25→
    26→
    27→def load_plan(path: Path | None = None) -> PlanModel:
    28→    """Load plan from disk, or return empty plan on missing/corruption."""
    29→    plan_path = path or PLAN_FILE
    30→    if not plan_path.exists():
    31→        return empty_plan()
    32→
    33→    try:
    34→        data = json.loads(plan_path.read_text())
    35→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as ex:
    36→        # Try backup before giving up
    37→        backup = plan_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    38→        if backup.exists():
    39→            try:
    40→                data = json.loads(backup.read_text())
    41→                logger.warning("Plan file corrupted (%s), loaded from backup.", ex)
    42→                print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex}), loaded from backup.", file=sys.stderr)
    43→                # Fall through to validation below
    44→            except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as backup_ex:
    45→                logger.warning("Plan file and backup both corrupted: %s / %s", ex, backup_ex)
    46→                print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    47→                return empty_plan()
    48→        else:
    49→            logger.warning("Plan file corrupted (%s). Starting fresh.", ex)
    50→            print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    51→            return empty_plan()
    52→
    53→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    54→        logger.warning("Plan file root is not a JSON object. Starting fresh.")
    55→        print("  Warning: Plan file root must be a JSON object. Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    56→        return empty_plan()
    57→
    58→    version = data.get("version", 1)
    59→    if version > PLAN_VERSION:
    60→        logger.warning("Plan file version %d > supported %d.", version, PLAN_VERSION)
    61→        print(
    62→            f"  Warning: Plan file version {version} is newer than supported "
    63→            f"({PLAN_VERSION}). Some features may not work correctly.",
    64→            file=sys.stderr,
    65→        )
    66→
    67→    ensure_plan_defaults(data)
    68→    try:
    69→        validate_plan(data)
    70→    except ValueError as ex:
    71→        logger.warning("Plan invariants invalid (%s). Starting fresh.", ex)
    72→        print(f"  Warning: Plan invariants invalid ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    73→        return empty_plan()
    74→
    75→    return data  # type: ignore[return-value]
    76→
    77→
    78→def save_plan(plan: PlanModel | dict, path: Path | None = None) -> None:
    79→    """Validate and save plan to disk atomically."""
    80→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    81→    plan["updated"] = utc_now()
    82→    validate_plan(plan)
    83→
    84→    plan_path = path or PLAN_FILE
    85→    plan_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    86→
    87→    content = json.dumps(plan, indent=2, default=json_default) + "\n"
    88→
    89→    if plan_path.exists():
    90→        backup = plan_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    91→        try:
    92→            shutil.copy2(str(plan_path), str(backup))
    93→        except OSError as backup_ex:
    94→            log_best_effort_failure(logger, "create plan backup", backup_ex)
    95→
    96→    try:
    97→        safe_write_text(plan_path, content)
    98→    except OSError as ex:
    99→        print(f"  Warning: Could not save plan: {ex}", file=sys.stderr)
   100→        raise
   101→
   102→
   103→def plan_path_for_state(state_path: Path) -> Path:
   104→    """Derive plan.json path from a state file path."""
   105→    return state_path.parent / "plan.json"
   106→
   107→
   108→def has_living_plan(path: Path | None = None) -> bool:
   109→    """Return True if a plan.json exists and has user intent."""
   110→    plan_path = path or PLAN_FILE
   111→    if not plan_path.exists():
   112→        return False
   113→    plan = load_plan(plan_path)
   114→    return bool(
   115→        plan.get("queue_order")
   116→        or plan.get("overrides")
   117→        or plan.get("clusters")
   118→    )
   119→
   120→
   121→__all__ = [
   122→    "PLAN_FILE",
   123→    "has_living_plan",
   124→    "load_plan",
   125→    "plan_path_for_state",
   126→    "save_plan",
   127→]
   128→

   131→def triage_phase_banner(plan: PlanModel) -> str:
   132→    """Return a banner string when triage stage IDs are in the queue."""
   133→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   134→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   135→    has_triage = any(sid in order for sid in TRIAGE_IDS)
   136→    if not has_triage:
   137→        return ""
   138→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
   139→    stages = meta.get("triage_stages", {})
   140→    completed = [s for s in ("observe", "reflect", "organize") if s in stages]
   141→    if completed:
   142→        return (
   143→            f"TRIAGE MODE ({len(completed)}/4 stages complete) — "
   144→            "complete all stages to exit. Run: desloppify plan triage"
   145→        )
   146→    return (
   147→        "TRIAGE MODE — review issues need analysis before fixing. "
   148→        "Run: desloppify plan triage"
   149→    )
   150→
